**Minneapolis**[^cite_note-15] is a city in [Hennepin County, Minnesota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hennepin_County,_Minnesota), United States, and its [county seat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County_seat).[^cite_note-GNIS-4] With a population of 429,954 as of the [2020 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_census), it is the state's [most populous city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Minnesota).[^cite_note-Census-2020-Profile-8] Located in the state's center near the eastern border, it occupies both banks of the [Upper Mississippi River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_Mississippi_River) and adjoins [Saint Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Paul,_Minnesota), the state capital of Minnesota. Minneapolis, Saint Paul, and the surrounding area are collectively known as the [Twin Cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twin_Cities), a metropolitan area with 3.69 million residents.[^cite_note-16] Minneapolis is built on an artesian aquifer on relatively flat terrain and is known for cold, snowy winters and hot, humid summers. Nicknamed the "City of Lakes",[^cite_note-Sturdevant-17] Minneapolis is abundant in water, with [thirteen lakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_lakes_in_Minneapolis), wetlands, the [Mississippi River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippi_River), creeks, and waterfalls. The city's public park system is connected by the [Grand Rounds National Scenic Byway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Rounds_National_Scenic_Byway).

[Dakota people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dakota_people) previously inhabited the site of today's Minneapolis. European settlement began north of [Fort Snelling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Snelling) along [Saint Anthony Falls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Anthony_Falls)—the only natural waterfall on the Mississippi River.[^cite_note-NPSfalls-18] Location near the fort and the falls' power—with its potential for industrial activity—fostered the city's early growth. For a time in the 19th century, Minneapolis was the lumber and flour milling capital of the world, and as home to the [Federal Reserve Bank of Minneapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Reserve_Bank_of_Minneapolis), it has preserved its financial clout into the 21st century. A Minneapolis Depression-era labor strike brought about federal worker protections. Work in Minneapolis contributed to the computing industry, and the city is the birthplace of [General Mills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Mills), the [Pillsbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pillsbury_\(brand\)) brand, [Target Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Target_Corporation), and [Thermo King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thermo_King) mobile refrigeration.

The city's major arts institutions include the [Minneapolis Institute of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minneapolis_Institute_of_Art), the [Walker Art Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walker_Art_Center), the [Minnesota Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minnesota_Orchestra), and the [Guthrie Theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guthrie_Theater). Four professional sports teams play downtown. Musician [Prince](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_\(musician\)) played at the [First Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Avenue_\(nightclub\)) nightclub. Minneapolis is home to the [University of Minnesota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Minnesota)'s main campus. The city's public transport is provided by [Metro Transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metro_Transit_\(Minnesota\)), and [the international airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minneapolis%E2%80%93Saint_Paul_International_Airport), serving the Twin Cities region, is located towards the south on the city limits.

Residents adhere to more than fifty religions. Despite its well-regarded quality of life,[^cite_note-19] Minneapolis has stark disparities among its residents—arguably the most critical issue confronting the city in the 21st century.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeber20224"The_overarching_goal_is_to_take_what_may_be_the_most_significant_issue_facing_contemporary_Minneapolis—the_crippling_disparities_among_its_people,_exposed_to_the_world_in_2020,_after_the_murder_of_George_Floyd—and_present_a_history_that_examines_why_those_disparities_exist,_even_as_the_city_makes_a_legitimate_argument_for_itself_as_a_must-see_or_must-live_kind_of_place."-20] Governed by a mayor-council system, Minneapolis has a political landscape dominated by the [Minnesota Democratic–Farmer–Labor Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minnesota_Democratic%E2%80%93Farmer%E2%80%93Labor_Party) (DFL), with [Jacob Frey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacob_Frey) serving as mayor since 2018.

## History

Main article: [History of Minneapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Minneapolis)

### Dakota homeland

Further information: [Dakota people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dakota_people), [Ojibwe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ojibwe), [Bdóte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bd%C3%B3te), and [Dakota War of 1862](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dakota_War_of_1862)

Two Indigenous nations inhabited the area now called Minneapolis.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELass200040-21] Archaeologists have evidence that since 1000 AD,[^cite_note-RFurst-22] they were the [Dakota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dakota_people) (one half of the [Sioux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sioux) nation),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWingerd2010365n-23] and, after the 1700s,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcConvellRhodesGüldemann2020560,_564"Finally_in_this_time_frame_other_groups_of_Ojibwes_began_pushing_to_the_west_and_southwest,_at_the_expense_of_the_Dakota_groups"-24] the [Ojibwe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ojibwe) (also known as Chippewa, members of the [Anishinaabe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anishinaabe) nations).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETreuer20103-25] Dakota people have different stories to explain their creation.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWestermanWhite201215-26] One widely accepted story says the Dakota emerged from [Bdóte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bd%C3%B3te),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWestermanWhite201215-26] the confluence of the [Minnesota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minnesota_River) and [Mississippi rivers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippi_River). Dakota are the only inhabitants of the Minneapolis area who claimed no other land;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeber20226-27] they have no traditions of having immigrated.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWestermanWhite20123–4"William_H._Keating,_a_geologist_who_came_to_the_Minnesota_area_on_an_exploratory_expedition_in_1823,_observed,_'The_Dacotas_have_no_tradition_of_having_ever_emigrated,_from_any_other_place,_to_the_spot_on_which_they_now_reside...'-28] In 1680, cleric [Louis Hennepin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Hennepin), who was probably the first European to see the Minneapolis waterfall the Dakota people call [Owámniyomni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ow%C3%A1mniyomni), renamed it the Falls of St. [Anthony of Padua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony_of_Padua) for his patron saint.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDeCarlo202015-29]

Island covered with hundreds of teepees

Dakota non-combatants interned at a [concentration camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concentration_camp) at [Fort Snelling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Snelling) during the winter of 1862

[^cite_note-Minnesota_Historical_Society_2015_w866-30]

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWestermanWhite2012194-31]

In the space of sixty years, the US seized all of the Dakota land and forced them out of their homeland.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWestermanWhite2012134,_136Page_136:_"Treaties_played_a_crucial_role_in_the_increasing_separation_of_the_Dakota_from_their_homeland_in_the_years_between_1805_and_1858,_leading_up_to_their_ultimate_expulsion_by_military_force_in_1863–64."_and_page_134:_"For_the_Dakota_the_word_''cessions''_might_well_be_replaced_with_''seizures''..."_and_"Collectively_these_treaties_included_three_great_cessions,_comprising_the_Treaties_of_1825,_1837,_and_1851"-32] Purchasing most of modern-day Minneapolis, [Zebulon Pike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zebulon_Pike) made the [1805 Treaty of St. Peter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_St._Peters#1805_Treaty_of_St._Peters) with the Dakota.[^cite_note-37] Pike bought a 9-square-mile (23 km<sup>2</sup>) strip of land—coinciding with the sacred place of Dakota origin[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWestermanWhite201215-26]—on the Mississippi south of Saint Anthony Falls,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStipanovich19824-38] with the agreement the US would build a military fort and trading post there and the Dakota would retain their [usufructuary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Usufruct) rights.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWingerd201077-39] In 1819, the [US Army](32087.md) built [Fort Snelling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Snelling)[^cite_note-40] to direct Native American trade away from British-Canadian traders and to deter war between the Dakota and Ojibwe in northern Minnesota.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWingerd201082-41] Under pressure from US officials[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWestermanWhite20124"government_officials_put_great_pressure_on_Dakota_leaders_to_be_quick_about_signing_a_treaty..."-42] in a series of treaties, the Dakota ceded their land first to the east and then to the west of the Mississippi, the river that runs through Minneapolis.[^cite_note-MNtreaties-43][^cite_note-55] Dakota leaders twice refused to sign the next treaty until they were paid for the previous one.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWingerd2010187,_193-56] In the decades following these treaty signings, the [federal US government](195149.md) rarely honored their terms.[^cite_note-57] At the beginning of the American Civil War, annuity payments owed in June 1862 to the Dakota by treaty were late, causing acute hunger among the Dakota.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBlegen1975265–267-58][^cite_note-60] Facing starvation[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnderson2019p._55:_"...they_had_to_beg_for_food_from_the_settlers_or_starve"-61] a faction of the Dakota declared [war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dakota_War_of_1862) in August and killed settlers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWingerd2010307The_uprising_involved_at_most_1,000_of_the_Dakota_population_of_more_than_7,000-62] Serving without any prior military experience, US commander [Henry Sibley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Hastings_Sibley) commanded raw recruits,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWingerd2010309-63] volunteer mounted troops from Minneapolis and Saint Paul with no military experience.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWingerd2010309,_314-64] The war went on for six weeks in the Minnesota River valley.[^cite_note-MNHSwar-65] After a [kangaroo court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kangaroo_court),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWingerd2010313"what_could_only_be_termed_a_kangaroo_court..."-66][^cite_note-71] 38 Dakota men were [hanged](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1862_Mankato_mass_execution).[^cite_note-MNHSwar-65][^cite_note-84] The army force-marched 1,700 non-hostile Dakota men, women, children, and elders 150 miles (240 km) to a [concentration camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concentration_camp) at [Fort Snelling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Snelling).[^cite_note-Minnesota_Historical_Society_2015_w866-30][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWestermanWhite2012194"The_remaining_seventeen_hundred_women,_children,_and_elderly,_including_hundreds_of_noncomabatants,_some_of_whom_had_protected_white_settler_refugees_from_the_war,_were_rounded_up_and_force-marched_to_a_concentration_camp_beneath_the_bluffs_of_Fort_Snelling...."-85] Minneapolitans reportedly threatened more than once to attack the camp.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWingerd2010320-86] In 1863, the US "abrogated and annulled" all treaties with the Dakota.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVogel2013540-87] With Governor [Alexander Ramsey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Ramsey) calling for their extermination,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnderson2019188-88] most Dakota were exiled from Minnesota.[^cite_note-89]

While the Dakota were being expelled, [Franklin Steele](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_Steele) laid claim to the east bank of [Saint Anthony Falls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Anthony_Falls),[^cite_note-90] and [John H. Stevens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_H._Stevens) built a home on the west bank.[^cite_note-91] In the [Dakota language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dakota_language), the city's name is *Bde Óta Othúŋwe* ('Many Lakes Town').[^cite_note-94] Residents had divergent ideas on names for their community. [Charles Hoag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Hoag) proposed combining the Dakota word for 'water' (*mni*[^cite_note-98]) with the Greek word for 'city' (*polis*), yielding *Minneapolis*. In 1851, after a meeting of the [Minnesota Territorial Legislature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minnesota_Territorial_Legislature), leaders of east bank St. Anthony lost their bid to move the capital from Saint Paul, but they eventually won the state university.[^cite_note-McKinney-99] In 1856, the territorial legislature authorized Minneapolis as a town on the Mississippi's west bank.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBaldwin1893a39-5] Minneapolis was incorporated as a city in 1867, and in 1872, it merged with St. Anthony.[^cite_note-100]

### Industries develop

Waterfall surrounded by mills and scaffolding

[Saint Anthony Falls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Anthony_Falls) c. 1850s

Two men loaded flour

Loading flour, [Pillsbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pillsbury_Company), 1939. Photo by [John Vachon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Vachon).

Minneapolis originated around a source of energy: Saint Anthony Falls, the only natural waterfall on the Mississippi.[^cite_note-NPSfalls-18] Each of the city's two founding industries—flour and lumber milling—developed in the 19th century nearly concurrently, and each came to prominence for about fifty years.[^cite_note-106] In 1884, the value of Minneapolis flour milling was the world's highest.[^cite_note-107] In 1899, Minneapolis outsold every other lumber market in the world.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKing200325–26-108] Through its expanding mill industries, Minneapolis earned the nickname "Mill City".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMinnesota_Historical_Society20031-109] Due to the occupational hazards of milling, six companies manufactured artificial limbs.[^cite_note-110]

Disasters struck in the late 19th century: the [Eastman tunnel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastman_tunnel) under the river leaked in 1869; twice, fire destroyed the entire row of sawmills on the east bank;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKane198781,_122-111] an explosion of flour dust at the [Washburn A mill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Mill_Disaster) killed eighteen people[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELieblingMorrison1966181-112] and demolished about half the city's milling capacity;[^cite_note-deBeaulieu-113] and in 1893, fire spread from Nicollet Island to Boom Island to northeast Minneapolis, destroyed twenty blocks, and killed two people.[^cite_note-114]

The lumber industry was built around forests in northern Minnesota, largely by lumbermen emigrating from [Maine](19977.md)'s depleting forests.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBlegen1975320-115][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELarson200715-116] The region's waterways were used to transport logs well after railroads developed; the Mississippi River carried logs to [St. Louis](27687.md) until the early 20th century.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELass2000173–174-117] In 1871, of the thirteen mills sawing lumber in St. Anthony, eight ran on water power, and five ran on steam power.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELarson2007146-118] Auxiliary businesses on the river's west bank included woolen mills, iron works, a railroad machine shop, and mills for cotton, paper, sashes, and wood-planing.[^cite_note-119] Minneapolis supplied the materials for farmsteads and settlement of rapidly expanding cities on the [prairies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prairie) that lacked wood.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELarson20077,_29-120] [White pine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_pine) milled in Minneapolis built [Miles City, Montana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miles_City,_Montana); [Bismarck, North Dakota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bismarck,_North_Dakota); [Sioux Falls, South Dakota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sioux_Falls,_South_Dakota); [Omaha, Nebraska](46159.md); and [Wichita, Kansas](114932.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELass2000173-121] Growing use of steam power freed lumbermen and their sawmills from dependence on the falls.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKane1987108"Another_factor_which_contributed_to_the_decline_of_sawmilling_at_the_falls_was_steam_power"-122] Lumbering's decline began around the turn of the century,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELass2000180-123] and sawmills in the city including the [Weyerhauser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weyerhauser) mill closed by 1919.[^cite_note-124] After depleting Minnesota's white pine,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERisjord2005\[httpsarchiveorgdetailspopularhistoryof0000risjpage130mode2up_131\]"By_then,_however,_the_pine_woods_were_virtually_exhausted"-125] some lumbermen moved on to [Douglas fir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douglas_fir) in the [Pacific Northwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Northwest).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELass2000180Here,_Lass_calls_the_lumbermen's_actions_as_cutting_at_a_"rapacious_rate",_and_calls_out_a_"rapacious_assault_on_the_coniferous_forests"_on_page_196-126]

Large computer terminal

[Seymour Cray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seymour_Cray) and colleagues began work on the [CDC 6600](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CDC_6600) *(pictured)* in downtown Minneapolis and completed the project in [Chippewa Falls, Wisconsin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chippewa_Falls,_Wisconsin), in 1963.

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrice200536-127]

In 1877, [Cadwallader C. Washburn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cadwallader_C._Washburn) co-founded Washburn-Crosby,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGray195432-128] the flour-milling company that became [General Mills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Mills).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDanbom2003283-129][^cite_note-130] Washburn and partner [John Crosby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Crosby_\(General_Mills\))[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELass2000162-131] sent Austrian civil engineer [William de la Barre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_de_la_Barre) to [Hungary](13275.md) where he acquired innovations through [industrial espionage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_espionage).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDanbom2003277-132] De la Barre calculated and managed the power at the falls and encouraged steam for auxiliary power.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKane1987118-133] [C. A. Pillsbury Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pillsbury_\(brand\)#Founding_and_early_development) across the river hired Washburn-Crosby employees and began using the new methods.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDanbom2003277-132] Wheat farming developed west across the [Great Plains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Plains), from Minnesota, to the [Dakotas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dakota_Boom) and Montana,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuhlmann192993-134] and new rail lines connected these farmers to the Minneapolis mills, reciprocally spurring further expansion.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDanbom2003272-135] The [hard red spring wheat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wheat_production_in_the_United_States#Classification_and_uses) grown in Minnesota became valuable, and Minnesota "patent" flour was recognized at the time as the best bread flour in the world.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDanbom2003277-132] In 1900, fourteen percent of America's grain was milled in Minneapolis[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDanbom2003277-132] and about one third of that was shipped overseas.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGray195441-136] Overall production peaked at 18.5 million barrels in 1916.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELieblingMorrison1966180-137] Decades of [soil exhaustion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soil_exhaustion), [stem rust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stem_rust), and changes in freight tariffs combined to quash the city's flour industry.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELass2000238-138] In the 1920s, Washburn-Crosby and Pillsbury developed new milling centers in [Buffalo, New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buffalo,_New_York), and [Kansas City, Missouri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kansas_City,_Missouri), while maintaining their headquarters in Minneapolis.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELass2000238"The_anticipated_decline_came_rather_abruptly_during_the_1920s._By_the_end_of_that_decade_the_Mill_City_produced_only_slightly_more_than_half_as_much_flour_as_it_had_at_its_zenith,_and_ranked_third_after_Buffalo_and_Kansas_City,_Missouri."-139] The falls became a [national historic district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_contributing_properties_in_the_St._Anthony_Falls_Historic_District),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKane1987186-140] and the upper St. Anthony [lock and dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lock_and_dam) was permanently closed to traffic.[^cite_note-141] The city announced that in accordance with a 2020 act of Congress, ownership of 5 acres (2.0 hectares) of federal land around the falls will transfer in 2026 to a Dakota-led nonprofit Owámniyomni Okhódayapi.[^cite_note-142]

Columnist Don Morrison says that after the milling era waned a "modern, major city" emerged.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELieblingMorrison196629-143] Around 1900, Minneapolis attracted skilled workers[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStipanovich1982104"Thus_while_Minneapolis_began_to_lose_jobs_in_the_mills,_it_began_to_acquire_other_jobs_in_management,_financial_administration,_advertising,_market_research,_product_research_and_design,_and_other_mid-level_management_and_administrative_positions._The_effect_was_to_upgrade_the_workforce..."-144] who leveraged expertise from the University of Minnesota.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStipanovich1982111"The_university's_role_grew_more_and_more_important_as_the_20th_century_rolled_along,_for_basic_research_and_experimentation_grew_more_complex_and_costly_and_as_time_went_by."-145] In 1923, [Munsingwear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munsingwear) was the world's largest manufacturer of underwear.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeber202274-146] [Frederick McKinley Jones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frederick_McKinley_Jones) invented mobile [refrigeration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refrigeration) in Minneapolis, and with his associate founded [Thermo King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thermo_King) in 1938.[^cite_note-147] In 1949, [Medtronic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medtronic) was founded in a Minneapolis garage.[^cite_note-148] [Minneapolis-Honeywell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minneapolis-Honeywell) built a south Minneapolis campus where their experience regulating [control systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Control_system) earned them military contracts for the [Norden bombsight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norden_bombsight) and the C-1 [autopilot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autopilot).[^cite_note-BabbageHoneywell-149] In 1957, [Control Data](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Control_Data) began in downtown Minneapolis,[^cite_note-BabbageCDC-150] where in the [CDC 1604](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CDC_1604) computer they replaced [vacuum tubes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vacuum_tube) with [transistors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transistor).[^cite_note-151] A highly successful business until disbanded in 1990, Control Data opened a facility in economically depressed north Minneapolis, bringing jobs and good publicity.[^cite_note-BabbageCDC-150] A [University of Minnesota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Minnesota) computing group released [Gopher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gopher_\(protocol\)) in 1991; three years later, the [World Wide Web](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Wide_Web) superseded Gopher traffic.[^cite_note-152]

panoramic view of Saint Anthony Falls and the Mississippi riverfront in 1915

The [Mississippi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippi_River) riverfront and [Saint Anthony Falls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Anthony_Falls) in 1915. At left, [Pillsbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pillsbury_%22A%22_Mill), power plants and the [Stone Arch Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stone_Arch_Bridge_\(Minneapolis\)). Today the [Minnesota Historical Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minnesota_Historical_Society)'s Mill City Museum is in the [Washburn "A" Mill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washburn_%22A%22_Mill), across the river just to the left of the falls. At center-left are [Northwestern Consolidated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwestern_Consolidated_Milling_Company) mills. The tall building is [Minneapolis City Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minneapolis_City_Hall). In the right foreground are [Nicollet Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicollet_Island) and the [Hennepin Avenue Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hennepin_Avenue_Bridge).

### Social tensions

Further information: [List of incidents of civil unrest in Minneapolis–Saint Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_incidents_of_civil_unrest_in_Minneapolis%E2%80%93Saint_Paul), [2020–2023 Minneapolis–Saint Paul racial unrest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020%E2%80%932023_Minneapolis%E2%80%93Saint_Paul_racial_unrest), and [Operation Metro Surge](82105033.md)

Group confronting police

[Battle between striking teamsters and police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minneapolis_general_strike_of_1934), 1934. The May *(pictured)* and subsequent July battles killed four men, two on each side.

[^cite_note-153]

In many ways, the 20th century in Minneapolis was a difficult time of bigotry and malfeasance, beginning with four decades of corruption.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeber202271-154] Known initially as a kindly physician, mayor [Doc Ames](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A._A._Ames) made his brother police chief, ran the city into crime, and tried to leave town in 1902.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENathanson2010\[httpsarchiveorgdetailsminneapolisintwe0000nathpage40mode2up_41–47\]-155] The [Ku Klux Klan](16779.md) was a force in the city from 1921[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHatleVaillancourt2009–2010362-156] until 1923.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChalmers1987\[httpsarchiveorgdetailshoodedamericanis00chalpage149_149\]-157] The gangster [Kid Cann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kid_Cann) engaged in bribery and intimidation between the 1920s and the 1940s.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENathanson2010\[httpsarchiveorgdetailsminneapolisintwe0000nathpage58mode2up_58\]-158]

During the summer of 1934 and the financial downturn of the Great Depression, the [Citizens' Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citizens'_Alliance), an association of employers, refused to negotiate with [teamsters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Brotherhood_of_Teamsters). The truck drivers [union](17626.md) executed [strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minneapolis_general_strike_of_1934) in May and July–August.[^cite_note-159] [Charles Rumford Walker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Rumford_Walker) said that Minneapolis teamsters succeeded in part due to the "military precision of the strike machine".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWalker193798–99-160] The union victory ultimately led to [1935](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Labor_Relations_Act_of_1935) and [1938](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fair_Labor_Standards_Act_of_1938) federal laws protecting workers' rights.[^cite_note-161]

From the end of World War I in 1918 until 1950, [antisemitism](1078.md) was commonplace in Minneapolis—[Carey McWilliams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carey_McWilliams_\(journalist\)) called the city the antisemitic capital of the US.[^cite_note-162] Starting in 1936, a fascist [hate group](437067.md) known as the [Silver Shirts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silver_Legion_of_America) held meetings in the city.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeber199188–89-163] In the 1940s, mayor [Hubert Humphrey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hubert_Humphrey) worked to rescue the city's reputation[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECaro2002440,_454-164] and helped the city establish the country's first municipal [fair employment practices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fair_Employment_Practices_Commission)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGarrettson199385"On_the_second_try,_the_Fair_Employment_Practices_Commission_(FEPC)_bill_passed_in_1948._It_was_the_'first_municipal_FEPC_bill_in_America'"-165] and a human-relations council that interceded on behalf of minorities.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEReichard199862-166] However, the lives of Black people had not been improved.[^cite_note-ab-167] In 1966 and 1967—years of significant [turmoil across the US](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long,_hot_summer_of_1967)—suppressed anger among the Black population was released in two disturbances on Plymouth Avenue.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENathanson2010\[httpsarchiveorgdetailsminneapolisintwe0000nathpage115mode2up_115\]Chapter_4:_Plymouth_Avenue_Is_Burning-168] Historian Iric Nathanson says young Blacks confronted police, arson caused property damage, and "random gunshots" caused minor injuries in what was a "relatively minor incident" in Minneapolis compared to the loss of life and property in similar incidents in Detroit and Newark.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENathanson2010\[httpsarchiveorgdetailsminneapolisintwe0000nathpage115mode2up_115\]-169] A coalition reached a peaceful outcome but again failed to solve Black poverty and unemployment.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENathanson2010\[httpsarchiveorgdetailsminneapolisintwe0000nathpage128mode2up_128–129\]-170] In the wake of unrest and voter backlash, [Charles Stenvig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Stenvig), a law-and-order candidate, became mayor in 1969, and governed for almost a decade.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeber2022139-171][^cite_note-FOOTNOTENathanson2010\[httpsarchiveorgdetailsminneapolisintwe0000nathpage129mode2up_129–134\]-172]

Brick school in winter

The [American Indian Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Indian_Movement)'s [Heart of the Earth Survival School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heart_of_the_Earth_Survival_School) in 1983

Protesters marching carrying signs

[General strike](82198050.md) march downtown in 2026

Disparate events defined the second half of the 20th century. Between 1958 and 1963, Minneapolis demolished "[skid row](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skid_row#Minneapolis)".[^cite_note-174] Gone were 35 acres (10 ha) with more than 200 buildings, or roughly 40 percent of downtown, including the [Gateway District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gateway_District_\(Minneapolis\)) and its significant architecture such as the [Metropolitan Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_Building_\(Minneapolis\)).[^cite_note-Hart-175] Opened in 1967, [I-35W](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_35W_\(Minnesota\)) displaced Black and Mexican neighborhoods[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeber2022132-176] in south Minneapolis.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECraig20239–10-177] In 1968, [relocated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Relocation_Act_of_1956) Native Americans founded the [American Indian Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Indian_Movement) (AIM)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeber2022141"Explaining_the_name,_\[\[Clyde_Bellecourt\]\]_remembered_Alberta_Downwind_saying_at_AIM's_founding:_''Indian''_is_the_word_that_they_used_to_oppress_us._''Indian''_is_the_word_we'll_use_to_gain_our_freedom"-178] in Minneapolis. Begun as an alternative to public and [Bureau of Indian Affairs schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Indian_boarding_schools), AIM's [Heart of the Earth Survival School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heart_of_the_Earth_Survival_School) taught Native American traditions to children for nearly twenty years.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavis2013193-179] A same-sex Minneapolis couple appealed all the way to the US Supreme Court but their marriage license was denied.[^cite_note-Mumford-180] They managed to get a license and marry in 1971,[^cite_note-Mumford-180] forty years before [Minnesota legalized same-sex marriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Minnesota).[^cite_note-181] Immigration helped to curb the city's mid-20th century population decline. But because of a few radicalized persons, the city's large Somali population was targeted with discrimination after [9/11](5058690.md), when its [hawalas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawala) or banks were closed.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeber2022158–159-182]

In 2020, 17-year-old [Darnella Frazier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darnella_Frazier) recorded the [murder of George Floyd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murder_of_George_Floyd);[^cite_note-183] Frazier's video contradicted the police department's initial statement.[^cite_note-184] Floyd, a Black man, suffocated when [Derek Chauvin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derek_Chauvin), a White Minneapolis police officer, knelt on his neck and back for more than nine minutes. Reporting on [the local reaction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Floyd_protests_in_Minneapolis%E2%80%93Saint_Paul), *The New York Times* said that "over three nights, a five-mile stretch of Minneapolis sustained extraordinary damage"[^cite_note-Stockman_(4_July_2020)-185]—destruction included a police station that demonstrators overran and set on fire.[^cite_note-Caputo_et_al_(30_June_2020)-186] Floyd's murder sparked international rebellions, mass protests,[^cite_note-187] and locally, years of [ongoing unrest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020%E2%80%932023_Minneapolis%E2%80%93Saint_Paul_racial_unrest) over racial injustice.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMitchell202244"Two_years_have_passed_since_Floyd_was_killed,_but_the_site_where_he_died...continues_to_be_contested_space—an_ongoing_site_of_protest—but_also_a_sacred_location"-188][^cite_note-MPRFloyd-189] As of 2024, protest continued daily at the intersection where Floyd died, now known as [George Floyd Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Floyd_Square), with the slogan "No justice, no street".[^cite_note-MPRFloyd-189] Protesters continued to ask for twenty-four reforms—many now met; a sticking point was ending [qualified immunity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qualified_immunity) for police.[^cite_note-MPRFloyd-189] Minneapolis gathered ideas for the square and through community engagement construction was scheduled for 2026, with substantial completion by 2027. Applications were being evaluated for a memorial called Peoples' Way.[^cite_note-190]

[Operation Metro Surge](82105033.md), a [Department of Homeland Security](58236.md) operation involving the [US Immigration and Customs Enforcement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Immigration_and_Customs_Enforcement) (ICE) and [US Customs and Border Protection](757040.md) began in December 2025, focused on detaining and deporting [undocumented immigrants](7124412.md).[^cite_note-Martin-191] Initially targeting Minneapolis and Saint Paul,[^cite_note-Martin-191] 3,000 agents were deployed in the state.[^cite_note-KAREclip-192] Grassroots activists took trainings to observe and record ICE actions.[^cite_note-193] In January 2026, ICE officers [killed Renée Good](82055905.md) in Minneapolis;[^cite_note-\:1-194] later in the month, Border Patrol agents [killed Alex Pretti](82203651.md).[^cite_note-195] Both were US citizens. Tens of thousands engaged in mostly peaceful protests.[^cite_note-196] Operation Metro Surge cost Minneapolis about \$700 million for December 2025 through April 2026,[^cite_note-197] including police overtime, and extensive disruption to businesses by federal agents—lost business revenue December through March was estimated at \$444 million.[^cite_note-198] Border czar [Tom Homan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Homan) announced the surge's end in February but in March more agents were in the state than before the surge.[^cite_note-KAREclip-192][^cite_note-199] While a preliminary injunction was denied, Minnesota, Minneapolis, and Saint Paul's ongoing lawsuit argues the surge violated the [10th Amendment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/10th_Amendment).[^cite_note-200]

## Geography

Main article: [Geography of Minneapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Minneapolis)

Further information: [Climate of Minnesota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_Minnesota), [Climate of Minneapolis–Saint Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_Minneapolis%E2%80%93Saint_Paul), and [Geology of Minnesota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geology_of_Minnesota)

Clouds reflected in lake

The city's largest lake, [Bde Maka Ska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bde_Maka_Ska)

[^cite_note-201]

The history and economic growth of Minneapolis are linked to water, the city's defining physical characteristic. [Long periods of glaciation and interglacial melt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glacial_history_of_Minnesota) carved several riverbeds through what is now Minneapolis.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright19903–4-202] During the [last glacial period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Last_glacial_period), around 10,000 years ago, ice buried in these ancient river channels melted, resulting in basins that filled with water to become the [lakes of Minneapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_lakes_in_Minneapolis).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright19904-203] Meltwater from [Lake Agassiz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Agassiz) fed the [Glacial River Warren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glacial_River_Warren), which created [a large waterfall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/River_Warren_Falls) that eroded upriver past the confluence of the Mississippi River, where it left a 75-foot (23-meter) drop in the Mississippi.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright199014-204] This site is located in what is now downtown Saint Paul. The new waterfall, later called Saint Anthony Falls, in turn, eroded up the Mississippi about eight miles (13 kilometers) to its present location, carving the [Mississippi River gorge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippi_River_gorge) as it moved upstream. [Minnehaha Falls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minnehaha_Falls) developed during this period via similar processes.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFremling200556–60-205][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright199014-204]

Minneapolis is sited above an [artesian aquifer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artesian_aquifer)[^cite_note-Emporis-206] and on flat terrain. Its total area is 59 square miles (152.8 square kilometers) of which six percent is covered by water.[^cite_note-207] The city has a 12-mile (19 km) segment of the Mississippi River, four streams, and 17 waterbodies—13 of them lakes,[^cite_note-208] with 24 miles (39 km) of lake shoreline.[^cite_note-209]

A 1959 report by the US [Soil Conservation Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soil_Conservation_Service) listed Minneapolis's elevation above [mean sea level](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mean_sea_level) as 830 feet (250 meters).[^cite_note-harms-210] The city's lowest elevation of 687 feet (209 m) above sea level is near the confluence of Minnehaha Creek with the Mississippi River.[^cite_note-usgs_elevations-211] Sources disagree on the exact location and elevation of the city's highest point, which is cited as being between 967 and 985 feet (295 and 300 m) above sea level.[^cite_note-214]

### Cityscape

The Minneapolis skyline rises to its highest point at the center of the image, with the three tallest buildings standing out against a clear blue sky. Before the skyline are trees, university buildings, and residential complexes.

The Minneapolis skyline seen from the [Prospect Park Water Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prospect_Park_Water_Tower) in 2014

### Neighborhoods

Main article: [Neighborhoods of Minneapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neighborhoods_of_Minneapolis)

See caption

Cyclists on [Midtown Greenway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midtown_Greenway) in [Midtown Phillips](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midtown_Phillips,_Minneapolis), one of the [83 neighborhoods of Minneapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neighborhoods_of_Minneapolis)

Minneapolis has 83 neighborhoods and 70 neighborhood organizations.[^cite_note-215] In some cases, two or more neighborhoods act together under one organization.[^cite_note-216]

Around 1990, the city set up the Neighborhood Revitalization Program (NRP), in which every one of the city's eighty-some neighborhoods participated.[^cite_note-NRPprimer-217] Funded for 20 years through 2011, with \$400 million [tax increment financing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax_increment_financing)[^cite_note-NRPprimer-217] (\$572 million in 2025),[^cite_note-inflation-US-34] the program caught the eye of [UN-Habitat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN-Habitat), who considered it an example of [best practices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best_practice). Residents had a direct connection to government in NRP, whereby they proposed ideas appropriate for their area, and NRP reviewed the plans and provided implementation funds.[^cite_note-NRPprimer-217][^cite_note-218]

The city's Neighborhood and Community Relations department took NRP's place in 2011[^cite_note-219] and is funded only by city revenue. In 2019, the city released the Neighborhoods 2020 program, which reworked neighborhood funding with an equity-focused lens.[^cite_note-neighborhoods2020-220] This reduced guaranteed funding, and several neighborhood organizations have since struggled with operations or merged with other neighborhoods due to decreased revenue.[^cite_note-221] Base funding for every neighborhood organization increased in the 2024 city budget.[^cite_note-budget2004-222]

In 2018, the [Minneapolis City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minneapolis_City_Council) approved the Minneapolis 2040 Comprehensive Plan, which resulted in a citywide end to [single-family zoning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Single-family_zoning).[^cite_note-223] *[Slate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slate_\(magazine\))* reported that Minneapolis was the first major city in the US to make citywide such a revision in housing possibilities.[^cite_note-224] At the time, 70 percent of residential land was zoned for detached, single-family homes,[^cite_note-225] though many of those areas had "nonconforming" buildings with more housing units.[^cite_note-226] City leaders sought to increase the supply of housing to create more [affordable housing](3531662.md) and to decrease the effects single-family zoning had caused on racial disparities and segregation.[^cite_note-227] The [Brookings Institution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brookings_Institution) called it "a relatively rare example of success for the [YIMBY](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YIMBY) agenda".[^cite_note-228] From 2022 until 2024,[^cite_note-229][^cite_note-230] the [Minnesota Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minnesota_Supreme_Court), the [US District Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_District_Court_for_the_District_of_Minnesota), and the [Minnesota Court of Appeals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minnesota_Court_of_Appeals) arrived at competing opinions, first shutting down the plan, and then securing its survival. Ultimately in 2024, the state legislature passed a bill approving the city's 2040 plan.[^cite_note-231] In the years following the 2040 Plan, rent increases slowed in Minneapolis relative to comparison cities; the [Federal Reserve Bank of Minneapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Reserve_Bank_of_Minneapolis) pointed out the difficulty of disentangling potential effects of the plan from other market factors.[^cite_note-fed-2040-232]

### Climate

Further information: [Climate of Minneapolis–Saint Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_Minneapolis%E2%80%93Saint_Paul)

Minneapolis has a hot-summer [humid continental climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humid_continental_climate) (*Dfa* in the [Köppen climate classification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification))[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPeelFinlaysonMcMahon20071639-233] that is typical of southern parts of the [Upper Midwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_Midwest). It is in USDA [plant hardiness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hardiness_zone) zone 5a.[^cite_note-234][^cite_note-235][^cite_note-236]  The Minneapolis area experiences a full range of precipitation and related weather events, including snow, sleet, ice, rain, thunderstorms, and fog. The highest recorded temperature is 108 °F (42 °C) in [July 1936](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1936_North_American_heat_wave). The lowest was −41 °F (−41 °C) in January 1888.[^cite_note-237]

The snowiest winter on record was 1983–1984, when 98.6 in (250 cm) of snow fell.[^cite_note-DNRsnow-238] The least-snowy winter was 1930–1931, when 14.2 inches (36 cm) fell.[^cite_note-DNRsnow-238] According to the [National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration](37876.md), the annual average for [sunshine duration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunshine_duration) is 58 percent.[^cite_note-239]

## Demographics

Main article: [Demographics of Minneapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Minneapolis)

The Minneapolis area was originally occupied by [Dakota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sioux) bands, particularly the [Mdewakanton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mdewakanton), until [European Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Americans) moved westward.[^cite_note-247] In the 1840s,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStipanovich198248-248] new settlers arrived from [Maine](19977.md), [New Hampshire](21134.md), and [Massachusetts](1645518.md), while [French-Canadians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French-Canadians) came around the same time.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStipanovich1982203-249][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStipanovich1982217-250] Farmers from [Illinois](14849.md), [Indiana](21883857.md), [Ohio](22199.md), and [Pennsylvania](23332.md) followed in a secondary migration. Settlers from New England had an outsized influence on civic life.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStipanovich1982214-251]

[Mexican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_people) migrant workers began coming to Minnesota as early as 1860, although few stayed year-round.[^cite_note-Anderson-252] [Latinos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_Americans) eventually settled in several neighborhoods in Minneapolis, including [Phillips](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phillips,_Minneapolis), [Whittier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whittier,_Minneapolis), [Longfellow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Longfellow,_Minneapolis) and [Northeast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast,_Minneapolis).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHACER199819-253] Before the turn of the 21st century, Latinos were the state's largest and fastest-growing immigrant group.[^cite_note-Anderson-252][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELeague_of_Women_Voters20027-254]

Immigrants from [Sweden](5058739.md), [Norway](21241.md), and [Denmark](76972.md) found common ground with the [Republican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republicanism) and [Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestant) belief systems of the New England migrants who preceded them.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStipanovich1982224–225-255][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStipanovich1982220–222,_224-256] [Irish](775859.md), [Scots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scottish_people), and [English](18803164.md) immigrants arrived after the Civil War;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEThe_Minneapolis_'76_Bicentennial_Commission197618-257] [Germans](152735.md)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStipanovich1982239-258] and [Jews](25955086.md) from [Central](5188.md) and [Eastern Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Europe), as well as [Russia](59510.md), followed.[^cite_note-Nathanson-259] Minneapolis welcomed [Italians](385155.md) and [Greeks](42056.md) in the 1890s and 1900s,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVecoli1981450-260][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESaloutos1981472,_474-261] and [Slovak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slovaks) and [Czech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czechs) immigrants settled in the [Bohemian Flats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bohemian_Flats) area on the west bank of the Mississippi River. [Ukrainians](145144.md) arrived after 1900,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStipanovich1982244–247-262] and Central European migrants made their homes in the Northeast neighborhood.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStipanovich198248,_241-263]

[Chinese](372983.md) began immigration in the 1870s and Chinese businesses centered on the [Gateway District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gateway_District_\(Minneapolis\)) and Glenwood Avenue.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMason1981a531,_533–534-264] [Westminster Presbyterian Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westminster_Presbyterian_Church_\(Minneapolis\)) gave language classes and support for [Chinese Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Americans) in Minneapolis, many of whom had fled discrimination in western states.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMason1981a540-265] [Japanese Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_Americans), many relocated from San Francisco, worked at [Camp Savage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camp_Savage), a secret military [Japanese-language](15606.md) school that trained interpreters and translators.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAlbert1981561"...Minneapolis_received_by_far_the_greater_share_(see_Table_30.2)._Camp_Savage_and_Fort_Snelling,_the_greatest_magnets_for_wives,_relatives,_and_friends_of_those_stationed_there,_were_more_accessible_from_Minneapolis_than_from_St._Paul"-266] Following World War II, some Japanese and Japanese Americans remained in Minneapolis, and by 1970, they numbered nearly 2,000, forming part of the state's largest [Asian American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_American) community.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAlbert1981558-267] In the 1950s, the US government relocated [Native Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Americans_in_the_United_States) to cities like Minneapolis, attempting to dismantle [Indian reservations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_reservation).[^cite_note-268] Around 1970, [Koreans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koreans) arrived,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMason1981c572-269] and the first [Filipinos](2298105.md) came to attend the [University of Minnesota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Minnesota).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMason1981b546-270] [Vietnamese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_people), [Hmong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hmong_people) (some from [Thailand](30128.md)), [Lao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lao_people), and [Cambodians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khmer_people) settled mainly in Saint Paul around 1975, but some built organizations in Minneapolis.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMason1981d582,_584,_586,_590-271][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMason1981d586,_588,_589-272] In 1992, 160 [Tibetan immigrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tibetan_people) came to Minnesota, and many settled in the city's Whittier neighborhood.[^cite_note-273] [Burmese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burmese_people) immigrants arrived in the early 2000s, with some moving to [Greater Minnesota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Minnesota#Regions).[^cite_note-274] The population of people from [India](7564733.md) in Minneapolis increased by 1,000 between 2000 and 2010, making it the largest concentration of Indians living in the state.[^cite_note-275]

The population of Minneapolis grew until 1950 when the census peaked at 521,718—the only time it has exceeded a half million. The population then declined for decades; after World War II, people moved to the suburbs and generally out of the Midwest.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeber2022113-276]

By 1930, Minneapolis had one of the nation's highest literacy rates among [Black](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_people) residents.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaylor198182-277][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESpangler1961\[httpsbabelhathitrustorgcgiptiduc1b538878seq98q1illiteracy_94\]"Minnesota_Negroes_had_the_lowest_illiteracy_rate_in_the_nation_during_this_period"_\[in_the_period_1885_to_1920,_3.4_percent\]-278][^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaylor200234<abbr_title="circa">c.</abbr><span_style="white-space\:nowrap;"> 1930</span>_"In_Minneapolis_only_1.7%_of_blacks_over_10_years_of_age_were_illiterate"-279] However, [discrimination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Discrimination_in_the_United_States) prevented them from obtaining higher-paying jobs.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaylor198176-280] In 1935, [Cecil Newman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cecil_Newman) and the *[Minneapolis Spokesman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minnesota_Spokesman-Recorder)* led a year-long consumer boycott of four area breweries that refused to hire Blacks.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaylor198184-281] Employment improved during World War II, but [housing discrimination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housing_discrimination_in_the_United_States) persisted.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaylor198190footnote_57-282] Between 1950 and 1970, the Black population in Minneapolis increased by 436 percent.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaylor198184-281] After the [Rust Belt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rust_Belt) economy declined in the 1980s, Black migrants were attracted to Minneapolis for its job opportunities, good schools, and safe neighborhoods.[^cite_note-283] In the 1990s, immigrants from the [Horn of Africa](62142.md) began to arrive,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMaruggiGerten2013-284] from [Eritrea](17238590.md), [Ethiopia](187749.md), and particularly [Somalia](27358.md).[^cite_note-residents-285] Immigration from Somalia slowed significantly following a [2017 national executive order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trump_travel_ban).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeber2022p._159:_"President_Donald_Trump's_executive_order_in_2017_banned_new_immigration_from_Somalia_and_several_other_majority-Muslim_nations._Just_forty-eight_people_came_to_Minnesota_from_Somalia_in_2018,_down_from_more_than_fourteen_hundred_in_2016,"_and_further_reading_p._187-286] As of 2022, about 3,000 Ethiopians and 20,000 [Somalis reside in Minneapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Somalis_in_Minneapolis%E2%80%93Saint_Paul).[^cite_note-287]

The [Williams Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Williams_Institute) reported that the Twin Cities had an estimated 4.2-percent [LGBT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT) adult population in 2020.[^cite_note-288] In 2023, the [Human Rights Campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Rights_Campaign) gave Minneapolis 94 points out of 100 on the Municipal Equality Index of support for the LGBTQ+ population.[^cite_note-289] [Twin Cities Pride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twin_Cities_Pride) is held every June.[^cite_note-290]

### Census and estimates

Minneapolis is the most populous city in Minnesota and the 46th-most populous city in the United States by population as of 2024.[^cite_note-291][^cite_note-292] According to the [2020 US Census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_census), Minneapolis had a population of 429,954.[^cite_note-2020-P1RACE-293] Of this population, 44,513 (10.4 percent) identified as [Hispanic or Latinos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanic_and_Latino_Americans).[^cite_note-2020-P2ETHNICITY-294] Of those not Hispanic or Latino, 249,581 persons (58.0 percent) were [White](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Hispanic_whites) alone (62.7 percent White alone or in combination), 81,088 (18.9 percent) were [Black or African American](2154.md) alone (21.3 percent Black alone or in combination), 24,929 (5.8 percent) were [Asian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Americans) alone, 7,433 (1.2 percent) were [American Indian and Alaska Native](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Indian_and_Alaska_Native) alone, 25,387 (0.6 percent) some other race alone, and 34,463 (5.2 percent) were [multiracial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiracial_Americans).[^cite_note-2020-P1RACE-293]

The most common ancestries in Minneapolis according to the 2021 [American Community Survey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Community_Survey) (ACS) were [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Americans) (22.9 percent), [Irish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_Americans) (10.8 percent), [Norwegian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian_Americans) (8.9 percent), [Sub-Saharan African](27067.md) (6.7 percent), and [Swedish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swedish_Americans) (6.1 percent).[^cite_note-2021-DP02-295] Among those five years and older, 81.2 percent spoke only [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language) at home, while 7.1 percent spoke [Spanish](26825.md) and 11.7 percent spoke other languages, including large numbers of [Somali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somali_language) and [Hmong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hmong_language) speakers.[^cite_note-2021-DP02-295] About 13.7 percent of the population was [born abroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_born), with 53.2 percent of them being [naturalized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naturalization) [US citizens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_citizens). Most immigrants arrived from Africa (40.6 percent), Latin America (25.2 percent), and Asia (24.6 percent), with 34.6 percent of all foreign-born residents having arrived in 2010 or earlier.[^cite_note-2021-DP02-295]

The ACS reported that the 2021 median household income in Minneapolis was \$69,397 (\$82,453 in 2025), similar to the US household median income of \$70,784 in 2021.[^cite_note-296][^cite_note-inflation-US-34] The median household income was \$97,670 for families, \$123,693 for married couples, and \$54,083 for non-family households.[^cite_note-Minneapolis-data-297][^cite_note-2021-S1901-298] In 2023, the median Minneapolis rent was \$1,529, compared to the national median of \$1,723.[^cite_note-299] Over 92 percent of housing units in Minneapolis were occupied.[^cite_note-2021-DP04-300] Housing units in the city built in 1939 or earlier comprised 43.7 percent.[^cite_note-2021-DP04-300] Almost 17 percent of residents lived in [poverty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty) in 2023, compared to the US average of 11.1 percent.[^cite_note-USCensusQuick2023-301] As of 2022, 90.8 percent of residents age 25 years or older had earned a high school degree compared to 89.1 percent nationally, and 53.5 percent had a bachelor's degree or higher compared to the 34.3 percent US national average.[^cite_note-USCensusQuick2023-301] US [veterans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veterans) made up 2.8 percent of the population compared to the national average of 5 percent in 2023.[^cite_note-USCensusQuick2023-301]

In Minneapolis in 2020, Blacks owned homes at a rate one-third that of White families.[^cite_note-WPdisparity-302] Statewide by 2022, the gap between White and Black home ownership declined from 51.5 percent to 48 percent.[^cite_note-Reformer-303] Statewide, alongside this small improvement was a sharp increase in the Black-to-White comparative number of [deaths of despair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deaths_of_despair) (e.g., alcohol, drugs, and suicide).[^cite_note-Reformer-303] The Minneapolis income gap in 2018 was one of the largest in the country, with Black families earning about 44 percent of what White families earned annually.[^cite_note-WPdisparity-302] Statewide in 2022 using inflation-adjusted dollars, the median income for a Black family was \$34,377 less than a White family's median income, an improvement of \$7,000 since 2019.[^cite_note-Reformer-303]

### Structural racism

Before 1910,[^cite_note-ab-167] when a developer wrote the first restrictive [covenant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Covenant_\(law\)#Exclusionarycovenants) based on race and ethnicity into a Minneapolis deed,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWalkerRamerDericksonKeeler20236"The_first_racial_covenant_in_Minneapolis_was_recorded_by_Edmund_Walton_in_1910..."-308] the city was relatively unsegregated with a Black population of less than one percent.[^cite_note-Kaul-309] Realtors adopted the practice, thousands of times preventing non-Whites from owning or leasing properties;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDelegardEhrman-Solberg201773–74"...the_Seven_Oaks_Corporation,_a_real_estate_developer_that_inserted_this_same_language_into_thousands_of_deeds_across_the_city."-310] this practice continued for four decades until the city became more and more racially divided.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWalkerRamerDericksonKeeler20235"...the_Mapping_Prejudice_team_showed_that,_prior_to_the_introduction_of_covenants_in_1910,_the_residences_of_people_of_color_were_dispersed_throughout_the_city,_yet_as_developers_added_thousands_of_racial_covenants_to_deeds_in_Minneapolis_until_1955,_the_city's_neighborhoods_became_increasingly_racially_segregated"-311] Though such language was prohibited by state law in 1953 and by the federal [Fair Housing Act of 1968](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_Rights_Act_of_1968),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDelegardEhrman-Solberg201775-312] restrictive covenants against minorities remained in many Minneapolis deeds as of the 2020s. In 2021, the city gave residents a means to discharge them.[^cite_note-313]

Minneapolis has a history of [structural racism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Societal_racism)[^cite_note-314] and has racial disparities in nearly every aspect of society.[^cite_note-Mpls2040-315] As White settlers displaced the Indigenous population during the 19th century, they claimed the city's land,[^cite_note-316] and Kirsten Delegard of [Mapping Prejudice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mapping_Prejudice) explains that today's disparities evolved from control of the land.[^cite_note-ab-167] Discrimination increased when flour milling moved to the [East Coast](89126.md) and the economy declined.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeber202284,_88-317]

The foundation laid by racial covenants on residential segregation, property value, homeownership, wealth, housing security, access to green spaces, and health equity shapes the lives of people in the 21st century.[^cite_note-318] The city wrote in a decennial plan that racially discriminatory federal housing policies starting in the 1930s "prevented access to mortgages in areas with Jews, African-Americans and other minorities" and "left a lasting effect on the physical characteristics of the city and the financial well-being of its residents".[^cite_note-319]

Discussing a [Federal Reserve Bank of Minneapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Reserve_Bank_of_Minneapolis) report on how systemic racism compromises education in Minnesota,[^cite_note-320] Professor [Keith Mayes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keith_Mayes) says, "So the housing disparities created the educational disparities that we still live with today."[^cite_note-Mayes-321] Professor [Samuel Myers Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samuel_Myers_Jr.) says of [redlining](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redlining), "Policing policies evolved that substituted explicit racial profiling with scientific management of racially disparate arrests. ... racially discriminatory policies became institutionalized and 'baked in' to the fabric of Minnesota life."[^cite_note-322][^cite_note-324] Government efforts to address these disparities included zoning changes passed in the 2040 plan,[^cite_note-325] and declaring racism a [public health emergency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_health_emergency_\(United_States\)) in 2020.[^cite_note-326]

### Religion

Further information: [Religion in Minnesota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Minnesota)

Church, tower, and cross

[Christ Church Lutheran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christ_Church_Lutheran_\(Minneapolis,_Minnesota\)) is one of the city's four [National Historic Landmarks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_National_Historic_Landmarks_in_Minnesota).

[^cite_note-nps-327]

Twin Cities residents are 59 percent [Christian](5211.md) according to a [Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center) religious survey in 2023-24. The largest groups are [Mainline Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainline_Protestant) (21%), [Catholic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic) (18%) and [Evangelical Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelical_Protestant) (16%).[^cite_note-Pew-religion-328] Settlers who arrived in Minneapolis from New England were for the most part [Protestants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestant), [Quakers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quakers), and [Universalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universalist).[^cite_note-religion-329] The oldest continuously used church, [Our Lady of Lourdes Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Our_Lady_of_Lourdes_Catholic_Church_\(Minneapolis,_Minnesota\)), was built in 1856 by Universalists and soon afterward was acquired by a French Catholic congregation.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMillett2007127-330] St. Mary's Orthodox Cathedral was founded in 1887;[^cite_note-331] it opened a missionary school and in 1905 created a [Russian Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Orthodox) seminary.[^cite_note-332] [Edwin Hawley Hewitt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edwin_Hawley_Hewitt) designed [St. Mark's Episcopal Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Mark's_Episcopal_Cathedral_\(Minneapolis\)) and [Hennepin Avenue United Methodist Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hennepin_Avenue_United_Methodist_Church), both of which are located south of downtown.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMillett200784-333] The nearby [Basilica of Saint Mary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basilica_of_Saint_Mary,_Minneapolis), the first [basilica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basilica) in the US and [co-cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Co-cathedral) of the [Archdiocese of Saint Paul and Minneapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archdiocese_of_Saint_Paul_and_Minneapolis), was named by [Pope Pius XI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Pius_XI) in 1926.[^cite_note-religion-329] The [Billy Graham Evangelistic Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billy_Graham_Evangelistic_Association) was headquartered in Minneapolis from the 1950s until 2001.[^cite_note-334] [Christ Church Lutheran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christ_Church_Lutheran_\(Minneapolis,_Minnesota\)) in the [Longfellow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Longfellow_\(neighborhood\),_Minneapolis) neighborhood was the final work in the career of [Eliel Saarinen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eliel_Saarinen), and it has an education building designed by his son [Eero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eero_Saarinen).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMillett2007159–160"Christ_Church_was_Saarinen's_last_building"_and_"the_addition_was_among_Eero's_last_commissions"-335]

Aligning with a national trend, the metro area's next largest group after Christians is the 34-percent [non-religious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion) population.[^cite_note-Pew-religion-328] At the same time, more than 50 denominations and religions are present in Minneapolis, representing most of the world's religions.[^cite_note-religion-329] [Temple Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_Israel_\(Minneapolis\)) was built in 1928 by the city's first [Jewish](15624.md) congregation, Shaarai Tov, which formed in 1878.[^cite_note-Nathanson-259] By 1959, a Temple of Islam was located in north Minneapolis.[^cite_note-CityHistory-336] In 1971, a reported 150 persons attended classes at a Hindu temple near the University of Minnesota.[^cite_note-CityHistory-336] In 1972, the Twin Cities' first [Shi'a Muslim](26961.md) family resettled from Uganda.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBarlowSilk2004\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksideTpnyr2Z0moCpgPA139_139\]-337] Somalis who live in Minneapolis are primarily [Sunni Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunni_Muslim).[^cite_note-338] In 2022, Minneapolis amended its noise ordinance to allow broadcasting the [Muslim call to prayer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adhan) five times per day.[^cite_note-339] The city has about seven [Buddhist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist) centers and meditation centers.[^cite_note-340]

## Economy

Further information: [Economy of Minnesota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Minnesota)
